# Independent title audit — themes and verse titles

**Status: Chapters 1–18 reviewed; the expanded audit and required regression/build proofs are complete.** This is a second, independent reading of every theme title and every verse title in en/ne/hi against its full theme span / own verse, descriptions, and surrounding part structure. `source/ch<N>.json` remains canonical. Browser/live verification was not run.

**Coverage so far:** Chapters 1–18 complete — 169/169 themes and 700/700 verse titles (869 title records, each read in English, Nepali and Hindi). Twenty-two title records were corrected after their verdict tables; 50 advisories / 0 blocking remain locked. Full regression/build results and limits are in the final summary below.

**Owner decision (2026-11-05):** Approved the six Chapter 1–2 proposals recorded below and authorized continuing the audit through Chapter 10 using best editorial judgment. The scope was subsequently expanded to Chapters 11–18, with the same correction authority. Verdicts must be recorded before each chapter’s canonical edits.

## Method and verdicts

- A theme title is judged against the entire range, its own description, its parts’ titles/descriptions and the verses—not just the opening verse. Each language is judged independently.
- A verse title is judged against that verse’s own Devanagari/IAST and its en/ne/hi literals. Every claim, including a name, action, number, or moral characterization, must be supported there; neighboring verses may explain context but do not supply a missing claim.
- **Pass** means no change proposed. **Fix proposed** is a finding, not permission to edit. **Needs owner wording** means no wording will be applied until Dhruba decides.
- The overlap queue is only a reading prompt. No title was failed merely for sharing no content word.
- Proposed wording below is editorial work-in-progress, not a primary source or authority. The JSON and verse remain canonical.

Dhruba approved this trilingual wording; it was applied to source/ch1.json on 2026-11-05. |

## Chapter 1 outcome and hold point

**57 title records read:** 54 pass; 3 approved fixes applied (two theme records and verse 1.37). The finding at 1.01–1.03 is the originally reported coverage defect. The 1.45–1.47 theme title is also narrower than its own description. At 1.37 the mismatch is localized to Nepali and Hindi “sin” wording; the English title expresses the verse’s explicit prohibition.

**Dhruba approved the three Chapter 1 proposals.** All three were applied to canonical source/ch1.json after the verdict table was recorded.

### Chapter 2 outcome

**91 title records read:** 88 pass; 3 approved fixes applied (themes 2.04–2.06 and 2.22–2.25; verse 2.60).

## Chapter 3 — 3.01–3.43

**Coverage:** 10 themes and 43 verse titles, read in en/ne/hi. Theme titles were assessed against their full ranges and descriptions; all part titles/descriptions and each verse’s Devanagari, IAST and three literals were read.

|---|---|---|---|---|
| 3.01–3.05 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Why action cannot be avoided<br>ne: कर्मबाट किन पन्छिन सकिँदैन<br>hi: कर्म से बचना क्यों संभव नहीं | — | The title expresses the chapter’s answer to Arjuna’s opening question: action is unavoidable; the description includes both disciplines and the guṇas. |
| 3.06–3.09 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Act without hypocrisy or attachment<br>ne: दम्भ र आसक्तिविना कर्म गर<br>hi: दंभ और आसक्ति के बिना कर्म करो | — | The span contrasts hypocrisy with controlled, unattached action performed as sacrifice; all three titles carry that instruction. |
| 3.10–3.13 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Sacrifice nourishes both gods and people<br>ne: यज्ञले देवता र मानिसलाई पोषण गर्छ<br>hi: यज्ञ देवताओं और मनुष्यों का पोषण करता है | — | Reciprocal nourishment is the throughline; the descriptions add the theft warning and sacrificial remnants without narrowing the title. |
| 3.14–3.16 | pass en/ne/hi | en: The wheel that sustains life<br>ne: जीवन धान्ने यज्ञचक्र<br>hi: जीवन को धारण करने वाला यज्ञचक्र | — | The food/rain/sacrifice/action cycle and the consequence of failing to follow it are gathered by the wheel title. |
| 3.17–3.21 | pass en/ne/hi | en: The free person still acts for the world<br>ne: मुक्त व्यक्ति पनि लोकहितका लागि कर्म गर्छ<br>hi: मुक्त व्यक्ति भी लोकहित के लिए कर्म करता है | — | The span moves from Self-contentment to unattached action for the world and the example set by Janaka and other leaders. |
| 3.22–3.26 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Lead by acting, not by unsettling others<br>ne: कर्मको उदाहरण देऊ, अरूलाई विचलित नपार<br>hi: कर्म का उदाहरण दो, दूसरों को विचलित मत करो | — | Kṛṣṇa’s own action as example, wise unattached action and not unsettling others are all represented. |
| 3.27–3.30 | pass en/ne/hi | en: The guṇas act; surrender the work to me<br>ne: गुणले कर्म गर्छन्; कर्म ममा अर्पण गर<br>hi: गुण कर्म करते हैं; कर्म मुझे अर्पित करो | — | The guṇas/doership teaching leads into offering action to Kṛṣṇa and fighting without fever; the title spans both. |

| 12.01–12.05 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Devotion or the unmanifest: the choice of path<br>ne: भक्ति वा अव्यक्त: मार्गको छनोट<br>hi: भक्ति या अव्यक्त: मार्ग का चुनाव | — | The title captures Arjuna’s comparison, the answer favouring personal devotion, and the distinct but valid unmanifest path with its greater difficulty for embodied beings. |
| 12.06–12.08 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Surrender and fix the heart on me<br>ne: समर्पण गरेर मन-बुद्धि ममा राख<br>hi: समर्पण करके मन-बुद्धि मुझमें रखो | — | Covers action-surrender, Kṛṣṇa’s deliverance from death/rebirth, and the instruction to fix both mind and intellect in him; “heart” is idiomatic, not a conflicting literal claim. |
| 12.09–12.12 | pass en/ne/hi | en: A ladder of practice ending in peace<br>ne: शान्तिमा पुग्ने अभ्यासको सोपान<br>hi: शांति तक पहुँचाने वाला अभ्यास का सोपान | — | The span’s graded alternatives move from practice through work and fruit-renunciation, then compare knowledge, meditation and renunciation; the title captures the concluding peace. |
| 12.13–12.16 | pass en/ne/hi | en: Compassion and self-mastery make a dear devotee<br>ne: करुणा र संयमले प्रिय भक्त बनाउँछन्<br>hi: करुणा और संयम प्रिय भक्त बनाते हैं | — | The title names compassion and self-mastery as the devotee’s portrait; each description adds the span’s remaining traits, including non-disturbance, freedom from expectation and renunciation of undertakings. |
| 12.17–12.20 | fix proposed en/ne/hi | en: Evenness and faithful devotion complete the path<br>ne: समभाव र श्रद्धाले भक्तिमार्ग पूरा हुन्छ<br>hi: समभाव और श्रद्धा भक्तिमार्ग पूर्ण करते हैं | en: Even-minded, faithful devotees are exceedingly dear<br>ne: समभावी, श्रद्धालु भक्त अत्यन्त प्रिय छन्<br>hi: समभावी और श्रद्धालु भक्त अत्यन्त प्रिय हैं | Fix proposed in all three languages. Verses 12.17–19 repeatedly say this devotee is dear, and 12.20 concludes that those who follow the immortal dharma with faith are exceedingly dear. The current title substitutes “complete the path” for that central repeated claim. Proposed wording names even-mindedness, faith and the explicit concluding status. |

### Chapter 18 outcome

**96 title records read:** all 18 themes and 78 verse titles reviewed. Two theme-title corrections and one verse-title correction are proposed in all three languages. The 18.29 number advisory is retained and documented as contextually cleared. Verdict tables precede canonical edits.


## Checkpoint summary — Initial Chapter 1–10 request (2026-11-05)

- **Coverage:** 100/169 theme titles and 414/700 verse titles reviewed in English, Nepali and Hindi; 514 title records total. At the time of this checkpoint, Chapters 11–18 had not yet been requested; the audit now continues in the expanded scope below.
- **Canonical edits:** 16 title records corrected in `source/ch<N>.json`, each only after its chapter verdict table was recorded. Chapters 8 and 10 had no edits. Generated outputs were rebuilt, never hand-edited.
- **Method:** Sanskrit Devanagari/IAST and the edition’s own en/ne/hi literals were the semantic authority for verse titles. Theme titles were read across their complete span, description and part structure. The literal was never altered to fit a title; neighboring verses did not supply a missing verse-level claim. Zero-overlap queues were prompts only, and passing titles were cleared.
- **Provenance:** no external or AI-corrected dataset was used as primary authority; all verdicts were based on this repository’s canonical JSON and its embedded source-language text and literals. The ledger records old and proposed titles, verdict, and reason.
- **Mechanical advisories:** the 50-advisory / 0-blocking worksheet lock remains unchanged. Chapter 8.23’s “two times” number advisory was manually reviewed and cleared as a contextual false positive: the verse contrasts the two outcomes, and the following verses state the paths. A trial Chapter 6 wording that explicitly named Kṛṣṇa triggered a name-presence advisory despite the clear first-person referent; it was rejected and the final title avoids that unnecessary name inference. Neither validator nor lock was weakened.
- **Regression/proof results at this checkpoint:** four regression tests pin the 16 corrected trilingual title records, ledger coverage through Chapter 10, PROJECT scope/provenance, and the report-only study-check pointer. `python3 build.py` and `python3 build.py --prove` passed for the Chapter 1–10 scope; the expanded Chapter 11–18 task still requires final regression and build proofs. Two consecutive full builds had byte-identical generated output across 42 files (SHA-256 `2504c66f7bb6e597a0470effc779efd761a8ae49f2c2b02a8e28661db3d19b61`). Full suite: 94 Python tests, 937 document assertions, 560 site-health checks, 9,658 study checks; title advisory baseline remains 50 / 0 blocking.
- **Verification limits:** browser tests were skipped because Playwright/Chromium is unavailable in this environment; no browser or live-site verification was performed, and the SEO proof was local-only. Pillow was missing, so the build did not repaint `img/share-art.jpg`. These are explicitly unverified, not passed.

## Final summary — Chapters 1–18 (2026-11-05)

- **Coverage:** all 169/169 theme titles and 700/700 verse titles reviewed in English, Nepali and Hindi (869 records). Each chapter has its own en/ne/hi verdict tables; every theme is judged against its full range and description, and each verse title against its own Sanskrit, IAST, localized literals and part description.
- **Corrections:** 22 title records were corrected in canonical JSON only after their verdict tables were recorded: sixteen in Chapters 1–9; one each in Chapters 12, 13 and 14; and three in Chapter 18. Chapters 8, 10, 11 and 15–17 had no source edits. Generated files were rebuilt, never hand-edited.
- **Contextual advisories:** the 50-advisory / 0-blocking lock is unchanged.
